Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I came in because I keep hearing this man’s voice at work, and it’s gotten to where I can’t just brush it off anymore. I’m a mechanic, so I’m around noise all day—air tools, lifts, engines, fans, the ventilation system—so at first I tried to tell myself it was just the garage sounds mixing together or someone talking on the other side of the bay.\n\nBut it’s clear. Like, really clear. It sounds like a man standing close to me, sometimes like it’s coming through the garage vents, and other times it’s like it’s coming from under the hood of a car I’m working on. And he says things like not to trust my husband, or not to touch certain cars. It’s not like a vague thought in my head—it sounds outside of me, like somebody is actually there saying it.\n\nIt’s been scaring me, honestly, and it’s making it hard to work because I’ll stop and look around, or I’ll hesitate before touching a car because I heard it tell me not to. I just… I don’t know what’s happening, but it’s starting to feel like I can’t ignore it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like the voice you're hearing is part of a larger plan or conspiracy, or that it knows things about you that others don't?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I know how it sounds when I say it out loud. I know it sounds far-fetched, and people keep giving me that look like I’m connecting things that don’t belong together. But yes, I do feel like it’s tied into something bigger.\n\nI’m convinced my husband and the shop owner have been using the EV chargers and the security cameras at the garage to keep track of what I’m doing. Not just, like, normal cameras watching the shop—more like they know where I am, what bay I’m in, what car I’m touching, even when I’m trying not to make it obvious. The chargers have those little lights and screens, and I keep thinking they’re not just charging cars, they’re sending information somehow.\n\nThey’ve both told me I’m wrong. They’ve shown me footage and said, “Look, there’s nothing there,” or “See, nobody’s watching you like that.” But that doesn’t really settle it for me, because it feels like they could just show me the part they want me to see. Or erase something. Or the voice knows things before anybody says them, so then when they say there’s nothing going on, it almost feels like that’s part of it too.\n\nI’m not trying to be difficult. I just… I can’t shake it. It feels too specific to be random.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something very unusual or unlikely was happening to you that others would probably not believe, even though it seems completely real to you?\n\nPatient: Yes. That’s basically what I’m trying to explain, but it sounds so unbelievable when I say it out loud that I almost don’t want to say it.\n\nI really do think my husband and the shop owner are tracking me somehow through the EV chargers and the security cameras at the garage. Like, not just normal cameras being there for the business—I mean it feels like they’re using them to know what I’m doing, where I am in the shop, what car I’m working on, what bay I’m standing in. The chargers especially bother me because they have those lights and screens, and I keep feeling like they’re doing more than just charging cars.\n\nAnd I know people would say that doesn’t make sense. They have said that. They’ve shown me footage and told me there’s nothing going on, that nobody’s following me or watching me that way. But when they show me things, I don’t feel reassured, because I keep thinking they could be showing me only the part they want me to see, or that there’s something I’m not being allowed to see.\n\nSo… yes, I know it sounds far-fetched. I do know that. But it still feels completely real to me, and I can’t make myself stop believing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things, felt sensations, or believed things that others don’t seem to experience or understand, outside of what you’ve already described?\n\nPatient: I mean, the main thing is the voice. That’s what really brought me in. It’s not like I’m just “thinking” something or getting a bad feeling. I hear it like an actual man is there, close by, like close enough that I’ll turn my head because I think someone walked up behind me.\n\nAt work it’ll sound like it’s coming through the garage vents sometimes, especially when the fans are going or the ventilation kicks on, and other times it’s like it’s coming from under the hood of the car I’m working on. That part really gets to me because I’ll be leaning in, focused on whatever I’m doing, and then I hear him clearly telling me not to touch that car, or saying I shouldn’t trust my husband.\n\nAnd it’s clear. That’s the part I keep trying to explain. It doesn’t feel like background noise or my imagination filling in words. It sounds like somebody standing next to me saying it out loud, and I know other people don’t seem to hear it or understand why I react to it. I’ll stop working and look around, and nobody else is looking up or acting like anything happened. That makes me feel… I don’t know, scared and embarrassed at the same time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something to be true that turned out not to be, or had a strong belief about something that others couldn’t understand or agree with, even when you felt certain about it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s happened, or at least that’s what everyone keeps telling me it is. I know I’ve already brought it up, but it’s hard not to, because it’s the thing I can’t let go of.\n\nI’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner are using the EV chargers and the security cameras at the garage to track what I’m doing. Like, they’ll say it’s just normal shop equipment, normal security, normal chargers, and they’ve even shown me footage and said, “See, there’s nothing there.” But that doesn’t make me feel better. It actually makes me more suspicious sometimes, because I start thinking, well, of course they’d only show me the parts that make it look harmless.\n\nI know it sounds far-fetched. I really do. I can hear myself saying it and I know how it must sound to someone else. But when I’m there, it feels so real. The cameras, the chargers, the timing of things… it all feels connected, like they know what I’m doing before I even say anything.\n\nSo yes, other people don’t agree with me. They tell me I’m wrong, that I’m reading into things, that there’s no proof. But I still feel certain about it, even when they try to show me there’s nothing happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had beliefs or ideas that you know are unusual, even for you, or that you realize most people wouldn’t understand or believe, but you still feel certain are true?\n\nPatient: Yes. I know I’ve kind of already said it, but… yes, that’s exactly what it feels like.\n\nI know it sounds far-fetched. I know most people would hear me say that my husband and the shop owner are using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track me and think, “That can’t be right.” And I get that, on some level. I can understand how it sounds from the outside.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, in the shop, and I see the cameras pointed certain ways, or the charger lights changing, or it feels like people know where I am or what I’m doing before I’ve told them… it doesn’t feel like a theory. It feels like it’s happening.\n\nThey’ve shown me footage and told me there’s nothing there, that it’s just normal security cameras and normal chargers, but I still don’t feel convinced. I keep thinking there are parts I’m not seeing, or that they’re only showing me what they want me to see. So yes, I know it’s unusual, even for me. I know it doesn’t sound believable. But I still feel certain that they’re using those things to keep track of me.\n\nDoctor: When you see the EV chargers or the security cameras, do you feel like you are noticing something that others can’t see, or are you interpreting something that you actually see in a way that feels different from how others understand it?\n\nPatient: I think it’s more that I’m seeing the same things everyone else can see, like the chargers are there, the cameras are there, the lights change, the angles are what they are… but I’m interpreting it differently than everyone else, I guess.\n\nLike, I’m not saying I see an invisible camera or something nobody else can physically see. If someone walked in the shop with me, they’d see the EV chargers on the wall and the security cameras mounted up there. But to me it doesn’t just look like normal equipment. It feels like there’s a purpose behind it, like the way they’re placed and when the lights come on or how people react afterward means they’re being used to track what I’m doing.\n\nAnd I know, I know how that sounds. I can hear myself saying it and it sounds… extreme. But I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner are using them that way. Even when they show me the footage and say, “Look, there’s nothing here, nothing weird is happening,” I don’t feel reassured. I just start wondering what they didn’t show me, or whether there’s another feed, or whether the charger data is part of it somehow.\n\nSo I guess, yes, it’s something I actually see, but it feels like I’m noticing the real meaning of it and everyone else is either missing it or trying to convince me it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that you yourself would consider completely impossible or that you know no one else would ever believe, even beyond what you’ve already described?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it completely impossible, because to me that’s the problem — it doesn’t feel impossible when I’m thinking about it. It feels like something people could do if they had access to the equipment and wanted to hide it.\n\nBut I do know that other people would probably say no one would believe it. I mean, if I heard someone else say, “My husband and the shop owner are using EV chargers and security cameras at the garage to track me,” I might think that sounds extreme too. I can admit that. It sounds far-fetched when I say it out loud.\n\nBut I’m still convinced that’s what’s happening. Even when they show me the security footage and say, “See, there’s nothing there,” I don’t feel like that proves anything. I start thinking maybe they’re only showing me certain footage, or there’s another way they’re doing it through the chargers, or I’m not being shown the part that would prove it.\n\nSo… completely impossible? I guess no, I don’t experience it that way. I know most people don’t believe me, and I know it sounds like something nobody else would accept. But I still feel certain they’re using the chargers and cameras to keep track of what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever taken something you saw, heard, or experienced and felt like it had a special or hidden meaning that only you could understand, even though others might see the same thing and not think anything of it?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s pretty much what happens with the chargers and the cameras. It’s not that anyone else would walk in and see some obvious sign, like a message written on the wall or something. They’d probably just see normal security cameras and EV chargers and not think anything of it.\n\nBut when I see them, especially in the shop, it feels like there’s more to it. Like the way the charger lights change, or which camera is angled where, or when someone seems to know something about where I’ve been or what I was doing — it feels connected. It feels like I’m seeing the meaning behind it, and everyone else is either not seeing it or pretending it doesn’t mean anything.\n\nI know that sounds far-fetched. I really do. I’ve had people tell me it’s just normal equipment, and they’ve shown me footage and said, “Look, nothing is happening, there’s nothing there.” But even then, I don’t feel like, oh okay, I was wrong. I start thinking maybe that isn’t the whole footage, or maybe the tracking is happening through the chargers, or maybe the cameras are only part of it.\n\nSo yes, I guess I do take those things — the chargers, the cameras, the footage — and feel like there’s a hidden meaning in them. To me it means my husband and the shop owner are using them to keep track of what I’m doing, even though other people keep telling me it’s nothing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that something you actually saw or heard in the real world — like a word on a sign, a line in a song, or something someone said — was meant specifically for you or was sending you a hidden message?\n\nPatient: Yes… kind of. Not like a song lyric on the radio exactly, or a billboard spelling out my name or anything like that. It’s more with things I actually see in the shop — the EV chargers, the security cameras, the way the lights come on or where the cameras are pointed, and then something someone says afterward.\n\nLike if my husband says something that sounds like he knows where I was standing, or the shop owner acts like he already knows what I was doing before I told anyone, it feels like that is meant for me. Like it’s not just a normal comment. It feels like a signal, or like they’re letting me know without saying it directly that they’ve been watching or tracking me.\n\nAnd I know it sounds far-fetched. I hear how it sounds when I explain it. But I’m still convinced my husband and the shop owner have been using the EV chargers and security cameras to track what I’m doing. Even when they show me the footage and say, “There’s nothing there, see?” I don’t feel like that settles it. I just think maybe they’re not showing me everything, or the chargers are part of it in a way the footage doesn’t show.\n\nSo yes, I guess I do feel like some real things I see or hear are carrying a hidden message for me — mostly that they know what I’m doing and they want me to know they know, without coming right out and admitting it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had the experience of feeling watched, followed, or spied on outside of work, like in your home or other places you go regularly?\n\nPatient: Outside of work… I mean, yes and no. It’s strongest around the shop because that’s where the EV chargers and the cameras are, and that’s where I feel like the setup is actually happening. But it doesn’t completely stay there, because if they’re tracking what I’m doing through that equipment, then it makes me wonder what they know when I’m not there too.\n\nLike at home, if my husband says something that seems too specific, or he asks about something I didn’t think he should know about, I start thinking, okay, how did he know that? Was it from the shop? Was it from the cameras? Was it from the chargers logging something? I know that probably sounds far-fetched, and I know people keep telling me there’s nothing there. They’ll show me footage and say, “See, nothing happened,” but that doesn’t really reassure me. It just makes me think maybe I’m not being shown the part that matters.\n\nSo I wouldn’t say I always feel followed everywhere, like someone is physically behind me on the street. It’s more this feeling that I’m being tracked or monitored, and that it can reach outside work because my husband and the shop owner are connected to it. Even when I try to tell myself it sounds extreme, I still feel convinced they’re using the EV chargers and the security cameras to keep tabs on what I’m doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that you yourself would describe as completely impossible or not based in reality, even to you at the time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I would say “completely impossible,” because… that’s the hard part. To me, when I’m in it, it doesn’t feel impossible. It feels like I’m putting pieces together that other people are dismissing.\n\nBut I can admit it sounds far-fetched. I know that. Like, if someone else told me, “My husband and the shop owner are using EV chargers and security cameras to track what I’m doing,” I might think, okay, that sounds like a lot. Especially because people have shown me the footage and said there’s nothing there, that nothing is happening, and they act like that should settle it.\n\nBut it doesn’t settle it for me. Even when I can hear myself and think, “This sounds like something people aren’t going to believe,” I still feel convinced there’s something going on. I start thinking maybe the footage they showed me wasn’t all of it, or maybe the part that matters wouldn’t show up plainly on the camera, or maybe the chargers are involved in a way people don’t understand or don’t want to admit.\n\nSo… completely impossible? No, I don’t experience it that way. But not based in reality? I guess there are moments where I worry that’s what everyone else thinks, and a small part of me can see why. I just can’t make myself feel like it’s nothing. To me it still feels real.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever looked at or heard something in the real world — like a sound, a light, a word someone said, or even something on a screen — and felt like it was secretly giving you a message or showing you something that only you were meant to understand?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, that’s exactly what I mean, but it’s hard to explain without it sounding like I’m making connections out of nothing. It’s not usually like a random word on a screen saying something mystical to me or anything like that. It’s more like I’ll see the EV chargers at the shop, or notice where the security cameras are pointed, or I’ll hear my husband or the shop owner say something afterward that feels too specific, and then it all clicks together in my head.\n\nLike, if I was standing near a certain bay or doing something by the chargers, and later my husband makes a comment that sounds like he knows where I was or what I was doing, I feel like that’s not just a normal comment. It feels like he’s letting me know, indirectly, that he knows. And the same with the shop owner — sometimes the way he acts, or the timing of what he says, feels like he already has information before I’ve told him anything.\n\nI know it sounds far-fetched. I really do. I can hear how it sounds when I say it out loud. But I’m still convinced they’ve been using the EV chargers and the security cameras to track what I’m doing. Even when they show me the footage and say, “There’s nothing there, see?” it doesn’t make me feel better. I just think, well, maybe that’s not the right footage, or maybe they’re not showing me everything, or maybe what’s happening with the chargers wouldn’t show up that way.\n\nSo yes, when I see those things or hear certain comments, it feels like there’s a hidden message in it — like I’m supposed to understand that they’re watching me, even if nobody will admit it directly."
